Ladies and gentlemen, the draft "Invest in Greece", which we are debating today, is called "development" precisely because it aims at growth. The development of the country, the overall recovery of the economy and the final exit from the crisis are at the heart of our government policy. For investors to come, the country should provide assurances of reliability, guarantee of smoothness, stability, speed and efficiency of procedures, and guarantee of good functioning of the market. In order for investors to come, there is not enough general call, such as what the previous government used to do, without any serious preparation, to do, there is not enough patches in the legislation, which solve one problem, to create another and leave the rest unresolved. In order for investors to come, there is not enough to have one, but there must be no problem that would put their investment at risk. That is why we do not insist on fragmentary regulations, but on global reforms in all areas of public administration, justice, labour, infrastructure and the market. With today's bill we improve the investment environment, we facilitate entrepreneurship, with many benefits for the economy and employment, we form a fertile and friendly climate, leaving behind the obstacles of the past. We are putting an end to end to endogenous factors, such as the conviction of corruption, limited research and innovation, delays in the administration of justice. We are dealing with all those pathogens that are an obstacle to attracting investment. This is how we will achieve the growth leap needed by the Greek economy. That is why the business world of the country - and not only - welcomes all the axes of the bill, since it has been put into public consultation. In particular, with this draft law we are promoting an integrated national redesign and simplification programme. We facilitate business installation in modern business parks. We illustrate business activity in a new Digital Charter. We establish a National Infrastructure Register by defining the hosting investment areas. We simplify public procurement and speed up the licensing and operation of new businesses. We are motivating the development of the building industry, facilitating private investment in public property. We are taking steps to combat the undeclared and undeclared work, but also to create many new and well-paid jobs. And with all this and so many more that it introduces this bill we are drastically reducing time and money, both for businesses and for the state itself. Ladies and gentlemen, we want the state to be backed up by entrepreneurs and businesses and not bureaucraticly toward them, in order for the economy to develop, to benefit society and to advance the country. We want it and we will do it, because we have and we will have the Greek society, the workers, the entrepreneurs. Because we have and will have all those who believe in a modern, productive, creative Greece. We will try it and, despite the difficulties, we will succeed. Because, as you know, there is nothing impossible for the man who will try. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-10-23-S1-commons.u509	Thank you, Mr President. Minister, joy in your courage that you're here from morning to 1.00 in the morning! Ladies and gentlemen, development laws have been tabled so far, but what we have not seen is development, which we in Achaea especially need. The government brings a multi-project that aims to shape a new development and investment landscape in the country. There are a number of changes worth attentioning. In my limited time, I will try to refer to some of them. We have, therefore, mainly reforms aimed at shaping a new entrepreneurial environment by putting an end to the pointless suffering of investors and to all the chronic diseases of the system that operate dissuasively. The promise made by our government to Greek citizens was for high growth and new jobs and this promise intends to keep it. Unfortunately, to date, investment laws, instead of acting as a development tool, have enclosed investors in demonstrable processes and delays by depriving them of valuable resources. The new legislation introduces a more flexible procedure. The building factor of 0.6 on strategic investments in industry is extended. There is currently an increased build-up rate of 0.6 for industries of over 200 million euros with over 200 employees per year. This rate is aimed at industries of EUR 30 million that create seventy-five new jobs. Also, in industries of EUR 25 million, which create fifty new jobs, since they are in business parks. It is planned to simplify licensing legislation by abolishing the system of nuisances and incentives for the establishment of enterprises. As international practice and experience show us, they contribute directly to reducing unemployment through the necessary construction works and creating permanent jobs. Moreover, there is a significant change in decision-making to create a business park. It will be adopted by common decision of the relevant Directors of the Ministry of Development and Investment and the Ministry of Environment and Energy. In short, a critical competence is transferred from the political leaderships of the ministries to senior management. This section is also a practical implementation of the government's intentions to establish a state of staff by transferring powers from political leadership to state officials. It is the dismantling of decisions, the nature of which is purely technocratic. According to the National Plan for the Planning and Simplification of Procedures, the general principles of business licensing are recast in a more business-friendly direction, a simplification of the institutional framework for the unobstructed exercise of economic activity. The abolition of supporting documents, codification and digitisation of procedures are key elements of the programme in order to enhance the quality of services. For the first time, there is a consistent political will to deal with the problem through a comprehensive project by setting up a project group and interministerial participation. The time requires intelligent solutions to complex problems. We all know that one of the main illiteracies of the Greek economy lies in the simple and transparent framework for the organisation of the area and the authorisation of investment. As engineer and representative of the Technical Chamber of Greece I am well aware that the Single Digital Charter is a proposal that the TEI has formulated and presented for two years. This proposal today takes flesh and bone. After a careful study of the proposal, the Government immediately introduces such an electronic solution to the issues of spatial planning, town planning and the licensing of each project. The Single Digital Charter will be the electronic basis of the country, which will gather all the criteria, all the critical geographical data associated with the exercise of investment or construction activity. Any person concerned will be able to click on any point on the map to obtain online all the information he needs on issues such as: current land uses, construction conditions, rhymotomy, agialos, forest areas, protected areas and archaeological sites. After completion of the country's realisation, the limits of all lands will be added. But that's not all. At the same time, the Single Digital Charter will be binding on the administration as well. Such a system will be a lever of pressure in order to operate in terms of transparency, consistency and, above all, law. For the first time, the governors and the administration will operate on the basis of the exact same data. Up to now, the geogeographic data has been scattered across various archives in towns, land registers and elsewhere, resulting in uncertainty among the person concerned as to the completeness of the image of a property, the need for much time and, of course, high cost. [[At this point, the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member hits a warning bell.]] It is characteristic that in the relevant index of the World Bank on Property Assurance Greece ranks a hundred and fifty-third in a total of ninety economies. Eleven procedures are needed to register property when the average of the wealthy economies of the OSCE is only 4.7. It is an innovative return work that will put an end to the suffering both of the citizens and of course of the investors. It is time for our country to come to the next day, to align itself with all the modern-day countries that have similar systems. At the same time, the creation of the National Infrastructure Register, which will operate in digital format, will provide access to information that will compile the image of the building or building. Its absence to date has led to confusion about the responsibilities of operators for the maintenance and control of public infrastructures and buildings. There are dozens of cases where responsibility goes between central administration, agencies, regions, municipalities, etc. Within a certain period of time all public bodies should declare in the register the infrastructure and buildings, maintenance and control of which fall within their competence. Before I finish my speech, I would like to make a reference to work. Many are wondering why the relevant provisions have a place in this bill. The answer is that they are the prerequisite for triggering the development process, attracting investment and creating jobs. They contribute to returning our country to European regularity by directly applying the rules of the European social acquis and the International Labour Organisation. Some people are trying to give the impression that they are reducing the protection of workers by these measures. Not only is it not reduced, but on the contrary it is increased. We confirm the structure and the basic principles of collective bargaining, with respect for the collective autonomy of the social partners. Sectoral, operational and national or local collective agreements may not contain working conditions which are less favourable to workers than those laid down in national general collective agreements. In addition, there are provisions setting out the dismantling of undeclared work and strengthening the protection of workers. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, by making an overall assessment, I express my confidence that the multi-project will change the image of the country at all levels, will create jobs, growth and wealth generation. According to the study by the Bank of Greece, since the provisions of the bill are applied to their fullness, the minimum impact on GDP will be five points in the next decade. Thank you.

On the contrary, questions are probably created for service and interests. Our country in the governance of SYRIZA has for the first time acquired a national development plan with an emphasis on the knowledge, innovation economy, taking seriously the dimension of the 4th industrial revolution and, of course, climate change. The bill circumvents the basic principles of integrated planning and territorial cohesion and does not harmonise spatial planning with development planning. Article 11 on mooring permits medium-term nuisance facilities in Attica, even if they are organised activity receptors, and it is known that Attica is a highly charged environmental area, for example, with regard to traffic, transport, noise, air pollution. The aim should be to decentralise economic activities. It is still a step backwards from the settings of Manos and Souflias, which is fortified under Article 25 of the 2014 Regulatory Plan, which supports new low and middle nuisance activities, but in mixed-use areas and already available building and industrial stock. Why is it not taken into account that regulatory also promotes the relocation of incompatible vehicles and polluting uses, especially from areas subject to protection status? After all, the European Union's policy on the urban environment encourages green, sustainable cities, health protection against air pollution and, of course, there are directives in the EU. in relation to noise. Also, we now have two specific articles, 207 and 208, concerning spatial planning. The mandatory pre-approval procedure in Article 207 shall be repealed. It legalizes any special spatial planning over existing designs in one area, while there is the power of n.4447/2016. As regards Article 208 to date on the basis of Law 4447/2016, a local spatial plan per municipality is drawn up and a presidential decree is required which regulates land use and construction conditions. But now, with the bill we are discussing, there is a fragmentation of space, not the integration of spatial planning and local spatial plan by communities, not by municipality, as envisaged by the 2016 Act.
